{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VUBJbhL9Bqim9yYnLcwejr","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Cyfluent — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-16\n     4→Developer website: https://www.cyfluent.com/\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Cyfluent is a cloud-based Electronic Health Record (EHR) platform developed, operated, and owned by Planned Systems International (PSI), a leading provider of mission-focused national security, health IT, clinical, training, and environmental services to the U.S. Federal Government. Cyfluent is headquartered in Arlington, VA (3717 Columbia Pike) with additional offices in San Antonio, TX and Columbia, MD, and international presence in Ho Chi Minh City (Vietnam) and Shanghai/Hangzhou (China). The company was founded in 2009. CEO is Terry Lin; Elaine Law serves as VP of Operations.\n     9→\n    10→Cyfluent is a small, niche vendor primarily focused on the federal government and small ambulatory practices. The product is used by 150+ clinical practices across the U.S. and is notably the system of record for HRSA's National Hansen's Disease (Leprosy) Program — one of the few commercial EHR systems with a Federal Authority to Operate (ATO). They hold a GSA IT Schedule 70 contract (with Health SIN), enabling federal procurement. The company has approximately 11 employees. Their clearinghouse has processed over $7B in claims and more than 125 million prescriptions have been transmitted through the platform. Cyfluent is EHNAC accredited (both HNAP and Cloud-Enabled Accreditation Program/CEAP) and deployed on Azure FedRAMP cloud with NIST SP-800-37 Risk Management Framework compliance.\n    11→\n    12→Cyfluent describes itself as a Microsoft-based software development firm aiming to become \"a global provider of Electronic Health Record (EHR) software as a service (SaaS) for medical communities and patients worldwide.\"\n    13→\n    14→## Product: Cyfluent\n    15→\n    16→CHPL IDs: 10072 (Version 3.2, certified 2019-08-13), 11546 (Version 3.3, certified 2024-12-11)\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→Cyfluent is a comprehensive, cloud-native outpatient EHR platform delivered as SaaS. It is not just an EHR module — it's a complete health services platform encompassing EHR, practice management, patient portal (PHR), telehealth, occupational health, health services management, and pharmacy fulfillment. The certified module appears to represent the whole product rather than a component of something larger.\n    21→\n    22→The platform is built on a modular web services architecture, meaning components can be configured independently. Cyfluent describes this architecture as allowing \"rapid component configurations and modifications to meet specific mission requirements/user needs\" — with custom health workflows configurable within one to four weeks.\n    23→\n    24→The product is broadly certified with 40+ ONC criteria spanning clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(15), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(9), EHI export (b)(10), patient access (e)(2)–(e)(3), public health (f)(1), (f)(4), (f)(5), and FHIR API access (g)(7)–(g)(10). SED intended users are described as \"Outpatient Clinic.\"\n    25→\n    26→### Users & Market\n    27→\n    28→**Primary users**: Physicians, clinicians, nurses, billing staff, and practice managers in outpatient/ambulatory settings. Patients access the system through the PHR portal (CyPHR). The product also targets federal government health programs with specialized occupational health and health services management modules.\n    29→\n    30→**Clinical settings**: Solo and small ambulatory practices (fewer than 3 physicians is the typical target), as well as federal health programs. Supports 8 medical specialties along with general family practice, urgent, and emergency ambulatory care. Explicitly mentioned specialties include cardiology, ophthalmology, podiatry, and pulmonology.\n    31→\n    32→**Notable deployments**: The flagship deployment is HRSA's National Hansen's Disease (Leprosy) Program — the system serves as their EHR of record on Azure FedRAMP. The platform supports 150+ clinical practices across 13 states (EHR) and 21 states (practice management).\n    33→\n    34→**Go-to-market**: Dual channel — direct sales to commercial practices (available online with \"Buy Now\" option) and federal government procurement via GSA IT Schedule 70 contract. Pricing starts at $49/month for entry-level, $199/month for certified EHR, $199/month for practice management, $395/month for professional suite.\n    35→\n    36→### Modules & Functionality\n    37→\n    38→The vendor's website describes five major service areas, each with detailed sub-capabilities:\n    39→\n    40→**1. Electronic Health Record (EHR) System — CyCHART**\n    41→The EHR module, branded CyCHART, is described on the vendor's EHR detail page (cyfluent.com/services-7) with six functional areas:\n    42→\n    43→- **Patient Management**: Patient summary page with configurable longitudinal data including Allergies, Prescriptions, Problems, Patient Health History, Family History, Personal Habits, Contacts, Patient Alerts, Body Measurements, previous Vitals, previous Encounters, Vision, and Quality Indicators. Supports complex medical community organization.\n    44→- **Clinic Processes**: Encounter documentation mimicking paper notes, all sections visible from one screen. Supports treatment pathways, chronic disease management, prescription management, CPOE, interoperable encounter documentation, clinical flowcharts, structured and unstructured medical notes, nutritional notes. Customizable templates that compose sentences/paragraphs from user selections. Multiple encounters can be opened simultaneously.\n    45→- **Lab Functions and Prescriptions**: Electronic orders and results management for prescriptions, labs, and procedures — all accessible from inside the encounter. eRX (branded CySCRIPT) with automated checks for drug contraindications, dosing alerts, and generics. Includes Electronic Prescribing for Controlled Substances (EPCS), DEA certified with two-factor authentication.\n    46→- **Telehealth**: Synchronous and asynchronous telehealth via PHR Portal. Can connect with remote home telehealth devices from vendors including Cardiocom, AMC, Authentidate, American Telecare, Bosch Healthcare, Visual Telecommunication Network, Viterion TeleHealthcare, and Qualcomm Life's 2net Platform.\n    47→- **Patient Records and Mobile App Interoperability**: Mobile app and PHR allow patients to manage medical information. Data integration technology supporting XML, HL7 FHIR, X.12, CCR, TXT, HTML, SQL, PDF, DICOM and other image formats. Supports clinical assessments, medication reconciliation, care plans, order management, Clinical Data Exchange (CDE), encounter documentation, physician web access, patient web access, and notes access. Can pull from devices including smart watches, portable sonograms, EKGs via Bluetooth, Wi-Fi, USB.\n    48→- **Custom Forms Builder**: Allows creating custom forms with discrete, reportable data for workflows not matching pre-existing templates.\n    49→\n    50→**2. Practice Management — CyMED / CyfluentPM**\n    51→Described on the Practice Management detail page (cyfluent.com/copy-of-electronic-health-record-system):\n    52→\n    53→- **Medical Billing**: Automated proof of filing, desktop payer status integration, real-time billing from integrated EHR, claims manager for A/R, single-button electronic claims submission through EHNAC-accredited clearinghouse (operated by Cyfluent themselves). Consolidated billing across multiple payers. Charge ledger displaying all patient billing information. Exit billing from scheduling window.\n    54→- **Appointment Scheduling**: Multi-provider, multi-location scheduling. Treatment program scheduling with benchmarks and decision points. Patient automated messaging (text/email) integration. Business rules for practice alerts.\n    55→- **Medical Resource Management**: Clinic supply chain and inventory management covering pharmaceuticals, medical supplies, medical equipment, medical gases, and biohazardous materials. RFID/barcode reader integration for large inventories. Inventory usage patterns associated with treatment patterns. Inventory alerts as business rules. Billing module handles purchasing.\n    56→\n    57→**3. Occupational Health Management and Compliance System**\n    58→Described on the Occupational Health detail page (cyfluent.com/copy-of-practice-management):\n    59→\n    60→- Record of work-related injuries and illnesses (OSHA compliance), integrating with the employee's complete longitudinal medical history\n    61→- Adaptive forms for standard and custom metrics on specific injuries/illnesses\n    62→- Hearing and other exams required by OSHA standards\n    63→- Annual posting of injury and illness summary data (continuous integration of examination results)\n    64→- 24-hour OSHA workplace fatality notification with compliance forms and deadline alerts\n    65→- Workplace medical records for employees (OSHA-compliant, HIPAA/Privacy Act compliant)\n    66→- OSHA recordkeeping and reporting requirements with multi-vector indexing and graphing\n    67→\n    68→**4. Health Services Management System**\n    69→Described on the Health Services Management detail page (cyfluent.com/copy-of-occupational-health-management):\n    70→\n    71→- **Medical Evacuation**: Supports personnel deployed at remote locations needing return for medical conditions, with configurable assessment procedures and evacuation protocols\n    72→- **Medical Clearance for Travel**: Examination and approval for deployment to higher-risk environments, automatic completion of approval checklists by travel location\n    73→- **Travel Immunization**: Integration of immunization records with travel requirements by global region\n    74→- **Mental Health and Substance Abuse**: Configurable evaluation templates for job-related mental health and substance abuse metrics, with data separated from EHR data per behavioral health regulations\n    75→\n    76→**5. Pharmacy Fulfillment**\n    77→Described on the Pharmacy Fulfillment detail page (cyfluent.com/copy-of-health-services-management-sy):\n    78→\n    79→- **Shipping**: Pharmacy shipping services with tracking, labeling, temperature-controlled packaging for sensitive medications\n    80→- **Inventory**: Pharmacy inventory management with stock control and supply chain optimization\n    81→- **Barcodes**: Barcode technology for medication tracking, dispensing accuracy, and verification\n    82→\n    83→**Named sub-products**:\n    84→- **CyCHART**: EHR/charting module\n    85→- **CySCRIPT**: E-prescribing module\n    86→- **CyMED**: Practice management software\n    87→- **CyPORTAL**: Patient portal\n    88→- **CyCLAIMS**: Claims management\n    89→- **CyPHR**: Personal Health Record (patient-facing portal at cyfluentphr.com)\n    90→\n    91→### Data & Content\n    92→\n    93→Based on vendor materials, the product stores and manages the following types of data:\n Cyfluent is headquartered in Arlington, VA (3717 Columbia Pike) with additional offices in San Antonio, TX and Columbia, MD, and international presence in Ho Chi Minh City (Vietnam) and Shanghai/Hangzhou (China). The company was founded in 2009. CEO is Terry Lin; Elaine Law serves as VP of Operations.\n\nCyfluent is a small, niche vendor primarily focused on the federal government and small ambulatory practices. The product is used by 150+ clinical practices across the U.S. and is notably the system of record for HRSA's National Hansen's Disease (Leprosy) Program — one of the few commercial EHR systems with a Federal Authority to Operate (ATO). They hold a GSA IT Schedule 70 contract (with Health SIN), enabling federal procurement. The company has approximately 11 employees. Their clearinghouse has processed over $7B in claims and more than 125 million prescriptions have been transmitted through the platform. Cyfluent is EHNAC accredited (both HNAP and Cloud-Enabled Accreditation Program/CEAP) and deployed on Azure FedRAMP cloud with NIST SP-800-37 Risk Management Framework compliance.\n\nCyfluent describes itself as a Microsoft-based software development firm aiming to become \"a global provider of Electronic Health Record (EHR) software as a service (SaaS) for medical communities and patients worldwide.\"\n\n## Product: Cyfluent\n\nCHPL IDs: 10072 (Version 3.2, certified 2019-08-13), 11546 (Version 3.3, certified 2024-12-11)\n\n### What It Is\n\nCyfluent is a comprehensive, cloud-native outpatient EHR platform delivered as SaaS. It is not just an EHR module — it's a complete health services platform encompassing EHR, practice management, patient portal (PHR), telehealth, occupational health, health services management, and pharmacy fulfillment. The certified module appears to represent the whole product rather than a component of something larger.\n\nThe platform is built on a modular web services architecture, meaning components can be configured independently. Cyfluent describes this architecture as allowing \"rapid component configurations and modifications to meet specific mission requirements/user needs\" — with custom health workflows configurable within one to four weeks.\n\nThe product is broadly certified with 40+ ONC criteria spanning clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(15), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(9), EHI export (b)(10), patient access (e)(2)–(e)(3), public health (f)(1), (f)(4), (f)(5), and FHIR API access (g)(7)–(g)(10). SED intended users are described as \"Outpatient Clinic.\"\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Primary users**: Physicians, clinicians, nurses, billing staff, and practice managers in outpatient/ambulatory settings. Patients access the system through the PHR portal (CyPHR). The product also targets federal government health programs with specialized occupational health and health services management modules.\n\n**Clinical settings**: Solo and small ambulatory practices (fewer than 3 physicians is the typical target), as well as federal health programs. Supports 8 medical specialties along with general family practice, urgent, and emergency ambulatory care. Explicitly mentioned specialties include cardiology, ophthalmology, podiatry, and pulmonology.\n\n**Notable deployments**: The flagship deployment is HRSA's National Hansen's Disease (Leprosy) Program — the system serves as their EHR of record on Azure FedRAMP. The platform supports 150+ clinical practices across 13 states (EHR) and 21 states (practice management).\n\n**Go-to-market**: Dual channel — direct sales to commercial practices (available online with \"Buy Now\" option) and federal government procurement via GSA IT Schedule 70 contract. Pricing starts at $49/month for entry-level, $199/month for certified EHR, $199/month for practice management, $395/month for professional suite.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nThe vendor's website describes five major service areas, each with detailed sub-capabilities:\n\n**1. Electronic Health Record (EHR) System — CyCHART**\nThe EHR module, branded CyCHART, is described on the vendor's EHR detail page (cyfluent.com/services-7) with six functional areas:\n\n- **Patient Management**: Patient summary page with configurable longitudinal data including Allergies, Prescriptions, Problems, Patient Health History, Family History, Personal Habits, Contacts, Patient Alerts, Body Measurements, previous Vitals, previous Encounters, Vision, and Quality Indicators. Supports complex medical community organization.\n- **Clinic Processes**: Encounter documentation mimicking paper notes, all sections visible from one screen. Supports treatment pathways, chronic disease management, prescription management, CPOE, interoperable encounter documentation, clinical flowcharts, structured and unstructured medical notes, nutritional notes. Customizable templates that compose sentences/paragraphs from user selections. Multiple encounters can be opened simultaneously.\n- **Lab Functions and Prescriptions**: Electronic orders and results management for prescriptions, labs, and procedures — all accessible from inside the encounter. eRX (branded CySCRIPT) with automated checks for drug contraindications, dosing alerts, and generics. Includes Electronic Prescribing for Controlled Substances (EPCS), DEA certified with two-factor authentication.\n- **Telehealth**: Synchronous and asynchronous telehealth via PHR Portal. Can connect with remote home telehealth devices from vendors including Cardiocom, AMC, Authentidate, American Telecare, Bosch Healthcare, Visual Telecommunication Network, Viterion TeleHealthcare, and Qualcomm Life's 2net Platform.\n- **Patient Records and Mobile App Interoperability**: Mobile app and PHR allow patients to manage medical information. Data integration technology supporting XML, HL7 FHIR, X.12, CCR, TXT, HTML, SQL, PDF, DICOM and other image formats. Supports clinical assessments, medication reconciliation, care plans, order management, Clinical Data Exchange (CDE), encounter documentation, physician web access, patient web access, and notes access. Can pull from devices including smart watches, portable sonograms, EKGs via Bluetooth, Wi-Fi, USB.\n- **Custom Forms Builder**: Allows creating custom forms with discrete, reportable data for workflows not matching pre-existing templates.\n\n**2. Practice Management — CyMED / CyfluentPM**\nDescribed on the Practice Management detail page (cyfluent.com/copy-of-electronic-health-record-system):\n\n- **Medical Billing**: Automated proof of filing, desktop payer status integration, real-time billing from integrated EHR, claims manager for A/R, single-button electronic claims submission through EHNAC-accredited clearinghouse (operated by Cyfluent themselves). Consolidated billing across multiple payers. Charge ledger displaying all patient billing information. Exit billing from scheduling window.\n- **Appointment Scheduling**: Multi-provider, multi-location scheduling. Treatment program scheduling with benchmarks and decision points. Patient automated messaging (text/email) integration. Business rules for practice alerts.\n- **Medical Resource Management**: Clinic supply chain and inventory management covering pharmaceuticals, medical supplies, medical equipment, medical gases, and biohazardous materials. RFID/barcode reader integration for large inventories. Inventory usage patterns associated with treatment patterns. Inventory alerts as business rules. Billing module handles purchasing.\n\n**3. Occupational Health Management and Compliance System**\nDescribed on the Occupational Health detail page (cyfluent.com/copy-of-practice-management):\n\n- Record of work-related injuries and illnesses (OSHA compliance), integrating with the employee's complete longitudinal medical history\n- Adaptive forms for standard and custom metrics on specific injuries/illnesses\n- Hearing and other exams required by OSHA standards\n- Annual posting of injury and illness summary data (continuous integration of examination results)\n- 24-hour OSHA workplace fatality notification with compliance forms and deadline alerts\n- Workplace medical records for employees (OSHA-compliant, HIPAA/Privacy Act compliant)\n- OSHA recordkeeping and reporting requirements with multi-vector indexing and graphing\n\n**4. Health Services Management System**\nDescribed on the Health Services Management detail page (cyfluent.com/copy-of-occupational-health-management):\n\n- **Medical Evacuation**: Supports personnel deployed at remote locations needing return for medical conditions, with configurable assessment procedures and evacuation protocols\n- **Medical Clearance for Travel**: Examination and approval for deployment to higher-risk environments, automatic completion of approval checklists by travel location\n- **Travel Immunization**: Integration of immunization records with travel requirements by global region\n- **Mental Health and Substance Abuse**: Configurable evaluation templates for job-related mental health and substance abuse metrics, with data separated from EHR data per behavioral health regulations\n\n**5. Pharmacy Fulfillment**\nDescribed on the Pharmacy Fulfillment detail page (cyfluent.com/copy-of-health-services-management-sy):\n\n- **Shipping**: Pharmacy shipping services with tracking, labeling, temperature-controlled packaging for sensitive medications\n- **Inventory**: Pharmacy inventory management with stock control and supply chain optimization\n- **Barcodes**: Barcode technology for medication tracking, dispensing accuracy, and verification\n\n**Named sub-products**:\n- **CyCHART**: EHR/charting module\n- **CySCRIPT**: E-prescribing module\n- **CyMED**: Practice management software\n- **CyPORTAL**: Patient portal\n- **CyCLAIMS**: Claims management\n- **CyPHR**: Personal Health Record (patient-facing portal at cyfluentphr.com)\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on vendor materials, the product stores and manages the following types of data:\n\n**Clinical data** (per EHR detail page and product pages):\n- Patient demographics and contact information\n- Allergies\n- Prescriptions / medication lists (including controlled substances)\n- Problem lists\n- Patient health history\n- Family history\n- Personal habits (social history)\n- Patient alerts\n- Body (fixedMatch) prop.fixedLength = fixedMatch[1] === \"true\";\n      const unicodeMatch = attrs.match(/Unicode=\"([^\"]+)\"/);\n      if (unicodeMatch) prop.unicode = unicodeMatch[1] === \"true\";\n      const precisionMatch = attrs.match(/Precision=\"([^\"]+)\"/);\n      if (precisionMatch) prop.precision = parseInt(precisionMatch[1]);\n      properties.push(prop);\n    }\n\n    // Extract Association[\"referentialConstraint\"];\n    const principalMatch = body.match(\n      /<Principal Role=\"([^\"]+)\"[^>]*>.*?<PropertyRef Name=\"([^\"]+)\"/s\n    );\n    const dependentMatch = body.match(\n      /<Dependent Role=\"([^\"]+)\"[^>]*>.*?<PropertyRef Name=\"([^\"]+)\"/s\n    );\n    if (principalMatch && dependentMatch) {\n      refConstraint = {\n        principal: {\n          role: principalMatch[1],\n          propertyRef: principalMatch[2],\n        },\n        dependent: {\n          role: dependentMatch[1],\n          propertyRef: dependentMatch[2],\n        },\n      };\n    }\n\n    associations.push({ name, ends, referentialConstraint: refConstraint });\n  }\n\n  return associations;\n}\n\nfunction extractEntitySets(): Array<{ name: string; entityType: string }> extractAssociations();\nconst entitySets = extractEntitySets();\n\nconst totalProperties = entities.reduce(\n  (sum, e) => sum + e.properties.length,\n  0\n);\nconst totalNavProps = entities.reduce(\n EHI Export Mechanism (b10 Attestation)\nThe export works through two methods:\n- **Single Patient**: Navigate to patient chart → scroll bar shortcut → \"Export Patient Data to CCD\" → downloads CDA-format file\n- **Bulk Export**: Utilities → Files → CDA Extract Configuration → set date range → \"Extract now\" → downloads CDA-format files\n\nThe export format is **C-CDA 2.2** (Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture). The vendor points to the HL7 C-CDA 2.2 specification at `https://build.fhir.org/ig/HL7/cda-ccda-2.2/` as the format documentation.\n\n### 2. Provider Portal API (SwaggerHub)\nA comprehensive API with four sections:\n- **FHIR v4.0.1** endpoint at `cyfluentphr.com/fhirapi/{facilityCode}/service-base-url` — Inferno-compliant, supports dynamic registration and bulk exporting\n- **oData** endpoint at `.../Secure/oData.svc` — Full database CRUD, exposing 296 entity types (the entire data model)\n- **REST** endpoints for pulling files (`File.ashx`), C-CDA documents, prescriptions, and orders\n\n### 3. OData Database Schema (Data Dictionary)\nThe OData metadata XML is effectively a full data dictionary, documenting 296 entity types with field names, data types, nullability, max lengths, and all relationships. Key patient-data entity categories include:\n\n**Patient entities (36)**: Patient, PatientAllergy, PatientAllergyReaction, PatientPrescription, PatientProblem, PatientVital, PatientBodyMeasurement, PatientContact, PatientDiet, PatientFamilyHistory, PatientInsurance, PatientMedication, PatientNote, PatientObGyn, PatientPainHistory, PatientSocialHistory, PatientVision, PatientAdvanceDirective, PatientBloodGlucose, PatientDiabete, PatientEligibilityRequest, PatientPulseOximetry, PatientRace, PatientEthnicity, PatientIdentifier, PatientCarePlan (seen in API code), PatientImplantableDevice (seen in API code), etc.\n\n**Encounter entities (43)**: Encounter (43 properties, 51 nav props), EncounterDiagnosi, EncounterPrescription, EncounterExam, EncounterHpi, EncounterChiefComplaint, EncounterEducation, EncounterPainDescription, EncounterObGyn, EncounterPregnancy, EncounterDsm, EncounterRiskFactor, etc.\n\n**Orders/Actions (25)**: ActionItem (49 properties, 16 nav props — includes lab orders, procedure orders, results, and care plans), ActionItemResult, ActionItemResultValue, etc.\n\n**Code sets (36)**: Full coded terminology tables for ICD, CPT, LOINC, SNOMED, allergies, medications, reactions, diets, advance directives, etc.\n\n### 4. API Client Source Code\nThe C# `Program.cs` from API.zip demonstrates a working CDA extraction utility that:\n- Logs in via API\n- Queries patients with encounters in a date range via oData\n- Calls `File.ashx?mode=dataccd` to pull C-CDA for each patient\n- Also queries individual data tables (allergies, problems, medications, vitals, prescriptions, social history, care plans, goals, health concerns, implantable devices, etc.) via oData\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe Cyfluent EHI export presents a complex picture. There are actually **two export pathways** documented:\n\n**Pathway 1: C-CDA Export (the certified b(10) mechanism)**\nThis exports patient data in C-CDA 2.2 format. C-CDA covers a defined set of clinical data sections. Based on the API client code's comments, the CDA export includes:\n- Patient demographics (name, sex, DOB, race, ethnicity, preferred language)\n- Smoking status\n- Problems\n- Medications\n- Medication allergies\n- Laboratory tests and values/results\n- Vital signs\n- Procedures\n- Care team members\n- Immunizations\n- Unique Device Identifiers (implantable devices)\n- Assessment and Plan of Treatment\n- Goals\n- Health concerns\n\nThis is essentially the **USCDI v1 data set** — the same content required for (g)(10) FHIR API certification. It does NOT cover:\n\n- **Billing data**: No claims, charges, payments, A/R, or insurance billing records are included in C-CDA. The product's CyMED/CyCLAIMS modules store extensive billing data (charge ledger, claims, electronic remittance, payer status).\n- **Encounter documentation beyond clinical summary**: The rich encounter documentation (43 entity types including HPI, chief complaints, exam sections with nested groups/items, pain descriptions, risk factors, DSM assessments, OB/GYN data, pregnancy tracking) is far more detailed than what C-CDA captures.\n- **Custom forms**: The product supports a custom forms builder (DDO — \"Dynamic Data Objects\") with 9 template entity types. This custom clinical data is not represented in C-CDA.\n- **Scanned documents**: The scanning module (ScanPage, ScanPredocument, ScanSegment, ScanSession) stores document images.\n- **Patient notes**: PatientNote entity (12 properties) — clinical notes beyond encounter documentation.\n- **Occupational health data**: Work-related injuries, OSHA compliance records, hearing exams.\n- **Health services management data**: Medical evacuation records, travel clearance, travel immunizations, mental health/substance abuse evaluations.\n- **Pharmacy fulfillment data**: Inventory, shipping, barcode tracking.\n- **Secure messages**: Patient-provider secure messaging (23 properties per message).\n- **Fax records**: Incoming and outgoing faxes with file associations.\n\n**Pathway 2: oData API (indirect access to full data)**\nThe oData API exposes all 296 entity types — the entire database model. However, this is documented as a general-purpose API for database CRUD, not specifically as the EHI export mechanism. The b(10) attestation specifically describes the C-CDA export as the EHI export format.\n\nThis is a classic case where the vendor has the technical capability to export everything (via oData) but has certified the C-CDA export as the b(10) mechanism, which only covers the clinical summary subset.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Primary export format**: C-CDA 2.2 (XML)\n- **Standard**: HL7 Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture Release 2.2\n- **Appropriateness**: C-CDA is a recognized clinical document standard, but it is a **clinical summary format** designed for care transitions. It is not designed to carry the full breadth of an EHR's data — particularly billing records, custom assessment forms, occupational health data, or pharmacy operations data. For Cyfluent's product scope (comprehensive EHR + PM + occupational health + pharmacy), C-CDA is a significant mismatch.\n- **Reconstructability**: A third party could reconstruct a clinical summary from the C-CDA export. They could NOT reconstruct the full patient record — billing history, encounter-level detail, custom forms, scanned documents, and specialty modules would be missing.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The oData metadata XML is an exceptional artifact — a machine-readable, complete schema of 296 entities with typed properties, nullability, max lengths, and relationships. This is unusually detailed compared to most vendors.\n- The SwaggerHub API documentation is well-organized with working examples, including JSON response samples and C# client code.\n- The API.zip provides a runnable code sample demonstrating the full extraction flow.\n- Export instructions are clear and step-by-step.\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- The b(10) attestation document is minimal — a one-page Word document with export instructions and a link to the C-CDA spec. It does not describe what data sections are included in the export or acknowledge any limitations.\n- No data dictionary specific to the C-CDA export content (e.g., mapping EHR fields to C-CDA sections/templates).\n- No sample export files provided.\n- The oData metadata is from 2017 (based on Desktop.zip timestamp). The 2018 API client has ~41 more entities, suggesting the schema has evolved. The current production schema likely has additional entities.\n- The FhirUnitTests.txt file referenced in the SwaggerHub docs returns 404.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**OData schema (de facto data dictionary):**\n- Granularity: Excellent. Field-level documentation with names, data types (Edm.String, Edm.DateTime, Edm.Boolean, Edm.Int32, Edm.Guid, etc.), max lengths, nullability, and precision.\n- Relationships: Fully documented via 438 associations with referential constraints.\n- Code sets: 36 entity types for coded terminology, though actual value set contents are not included (they're data, not schema).\n- No field-level descriptions or business logic documentation.\n\n**C-CDA export documentation:**\n- Points to the external C-CDA 2.2 specification — no Cyfluent-specific mapping guide.\n- No documentation of which C-CDA templates are populated or which optional sections are included.\n- No sample export files.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Registered URL: https://www.cyfluent.com/onc-certified-hit-2015-edition\n- Final URL (after redirect): https://www.cyfluent.com/onc-certified-health-it\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: yes (Wix SPA for initial page, but linked documents accessible via curl)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (main page has direct links to CURES directory)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- The registered URL redirects (301) to a slightly different path (`/onc-certified-health-it` instead of `/onc-certified-hit-2015-edition`)\n- The Wix-hosted page requires JavaScript to render content (curl returns empty-looking HTML)\n- `FhirUnitTests.txt` referenced in SwaggerHub docs returns 404 at `https://www.cyfluentphr.com/_files/CURES/FhirUnitTests.txt`\n- The EULA PDF referenced in the SwaggerHub license field also returns 404\n- Parent directory `https://www.cyfluentphr.com/_Files/` returns 403 Forbidden\n- The oData metadata XML (Desktop.zip) dates from 2017, pre-dating the current version 3.3. The C# connected reference from 2018 has ~41 additional entity classes, suggesting schema evolution.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":243,"cache_read_input_tokens":107508,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":243},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"86127f38-4ef3-4bce-a151-c629fad8fa1e","uuid":"1f79499c-8cef-41b5-a221-37766ea842b0"}

Key patient-data entity categories include:\n\n**Patient entities (36)**: Patient, PatientAllergy, PatientAllergyReaction, PatientPrescription, PatientProblem, PatientVital, PatientBodyMeasurement, PatientContact, PatientDiet, PatientFamilyHistory, PatientInsurance, PatientMedication, PatientNote, PatientObGyn, PatientPainHistory, PatientSocialHistory, PatientVision, PatientAdvanceDirective, PatientBloodGlucose, PatientDiabete, PatientEligibilityRequest, PatientPulseOximetry, PatientRace, PatientEthnicity, PatientIdentifier, PatientCarePlan (seen in API code), PatientImplantableDevice (seen in API code), etc.\n\n**Encounter entities (43)**: Encounter (43 properties, 51 nav props), EncounterDiagnosi, EncounterPrescription, EncounterExam, EncounterHpi, EncounterChiefComplaint, EncounterEducation, EncounterPainDescription, EncounterObGyn, EncounterPregnancy, EncounterDsm, EncounterRiskFactor, etc.\n\n**Orders/Actions (25)**: ActionItem (49 properties, 16 nav props — includes lab orders, procedure orders, results, and care plans), ActionItemResult, ActionItemResultValue, etc.\n\n**Code sets (36)**: Full coded terminology tables for ICD, CPT, LOINC, SNOMED, allergies, medications, reactions, diets, advance directives, etc.\n\n### 4. API Client Source Code\nThe C# `Program.cs` from API.zip demonstrates a working CDA extraction utility that:\n- Logs in via API\n- Queries patients with encounters in a date range via oData\n- Calls `File.ashx?mode=dataccd` to pull C-CDA for each patient\n- Also queries individual data tables (allergies, problems, medications, vitals, prescriptions, social history, care plans, goals, health concerns, implantable devices, etc.) via oData\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe Cyfluent EHI export presents a complex picture. There are actually **two export pathways** documented:\n\n**Pathway 1: C-CDA Export (the certified b(10) mechanism)**\nThis exports patient data in C-CDA 2.2 format. C-CDA covers a defined set of clinical data sections. Based on the API client code's comments, the CDA export includes:\n- Patient demographics (name, sex, DOB, race, ethnicity, preferred language)\n- Smoking status\n- Problems\n- Medications\n- Medication allergies\n- Laboratory tests and values/results\n- Vital signs\n- Procedures\n- Care team members\n- Immunizations\n- Unique Device Identifiers (implantable devices)\n- Assessment and Plan of Treatment\n- Goals\n- Health concerns\n\nThis is essentially the **USCDI v1 data set** — the same content required for (g)(10) FHIR API certification. It does NOT cover:\n\n- **Billing data**: No claims, charges, payments, A/R, or insurance billing records are included in C-CDA. The product's CyMED/CyCLAIMS modules store extensive billing data (charge ledger, claims, electronic remittance, payer status).\n- **Encounter documentation beyond clinical summary**: The rich encounter documentation (43 entity types including HPI, chief complaints, exam sections with nested groups/items, pain descriptions, risk factors, DSM assessments, OB/GYN data, pregnancy tracking) is far more detailed than what C-CDA captures.\n- **Custom forms**: The product supports a custom forms builder (DDO — \"Dynamic Data Objects\") with 9 template entity types. This custom clinical data is not represented in C-CDA.\n- **Scanned documents**: The scanning module (ScanPage, ScanPredocument, ScanSegment, ScanSession) stores document images.\n- **Patient notes**: PatientNote entity (12 properties) — clinical notes beyond encounter documentation.\n- **Occupational health data**: Work-related injuries, OSHA compliance records, hearing exams.\n- **Health services management data**: Medical evacuation records, travel clearance, travel immunizations, mental health/substance abuse evaluations.\n- **Pharmacy fulfillment data**: Inventory, shipping, barcode tracking.\n- **Secure messages**: Patient-provider secure messaging (23 properties per message).\n- **Fax records**: Incoming and outgoing faxes with file associations.\n\n**Pathway 2: oData API (indirect access to full data)**\nThe oData API exposes all 296 entity types — the entire database model. However, this is documented as a general-purpose API for database CRUD, not specifically as the EHI export mechanism. The b(10) attestation specifically describes the C-CDA export as the EHI export format.\n\nThis is a classic case where the vendor has the technical capability to export everything (via oData) but has certified the C-CDA export as the b(10) mechanism, which only covers the clinical summary subset.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Primary export format**: C-CDA 2.2 (XML)\n- **Standard**: HL7 Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture Release 2.2\n- **Appropriateness**: C-CDA is a recognized clinical document standard, but it is a **clinical summary format** designed for care transitions. It is not designed to carry the full breadth of an EHR's data — particularly billing records, custom assessment forms, occupational health data, or pharmacy operations data. For Cyfluent's product scope (comprehensive EHR + PM + occupational health + pharmacy), C-CDA is a significant mismatch.\n- **Reconstructability**: A third party could reconstruct a clinical summary from the C-CDA export. They could NOT reconstruct the full patient record — billing history, encounter-level detail, custom forms, scanned documents, and specialty modules would be missing.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The oData metadata XML is an exceptional artifact — a machine-readable, complete schema of 296 entities with typed properties, nullability, max lengths, and relationships. This is unusually detailed compared to most vendors.\n- The SwaggerHub API documentation is well-organized with working examples, including JSON response samples and C# client code.\n- The API.zip provides a runnable code sample demonstrating the full extraction flow.\n- Export instructions are clear and step-by-step.\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- The b(10) attestation document is minimal — a one-page Word document with export instructions and a link to the C-CDA spec. It does not describe what data sections are included in the export or acknowledge any limitations.\n- No data dictionary specific to the C-CDA export content (e.g., mapping EHR fields to C-CDA sections/templates).\n- No sample export files provided.\n- The oData metadata is from 2017 (based on Desktop.zip timestamp). The 2018 API client has ~41 more entities, suggesting the schema has evolved. The current production schema likely has additional entities.\n- The FhirUnitTests.txt file referenced in the SwaggerHub docs returns 404.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**OData schema (de facto data dictionary):**\n- Granularity: Excellent. Field-level documentation with names, data types (Edm.String, Edm.DateTime, Edm.Boolean, Edm.Int32, Edm.Guid, etc.), max lengths, nullability, and precision.\n- Relationships: Fully documented via 438 associations with referential constraints.\n- Code sets: 36 entity types for coded terminology, though actual value set contents are not included (they're data, not schema).\n- No field-level descriptions or business logic documentation.\n\n**C-CDA export documentation:**\n- Points to the external C-CDA 2.2 specification — no Cyfluent-specific mapping guide.\n- No documentation of which C-CDA templates are populated or which optional sections are included.\n- No sample export files.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Registered URL: https://www.cyfluent.com/onc-certified-hit-2015-edition\n- Final URL (after redirect): https://www.cyfluent.com/onc-certified-health-it\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: yes (Wix SPA for initial page, but linked documents accessible via curl)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (main page has direct links to CURES directory)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- The registered URL redirects (301) to a slightly different path (`/onc-certified-health-it` instead of `/onc-certified-hit-2015-edition`)\n- The Wix-hosted page requires JavaScript to render content (curl returns empty-looking HTML)\n- `FhirUnitTests.txt` referenced in SwaggerHub docs returns 404 at `https://www.cyfluentphr.com/_files/CURES/FhirUnitTests.txt`\n- The EULA PDF referenced in the SwaggerHub license field also returns 404\n- Parent directory `https://www.cyfluentphr.com/_Files/` returns 403 Forbidden\n- The oData metadata XML (Desktop.zip) dates from 2017, pre-dating the current version 3.3.
